# Changelog

All notable changes to `vinceamstoutz/symfony-security-auditor` are documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning 2.0.0](https://semver.org). See [`docs/versioning.md`](docs/versioning.md) for the backward compatibility policy — what is public API, what is internal, and how deprecations are handled.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **The gate and report flags `--min-score`, `--fail-on-incomplete`, `--format` and `--output` now have a configuration key, and a flag always wins over it.** Their only home was the command line, so a repository could not keep its CI gate or its report format in YAML and every pipeline repeated the flags, while `--fail-on`, `--baseline`, `--no-cache` and `--path` already had `audit.fail_on`, `audit.baseline`, `cache.enabled` and `scan.included_paths`. `AuditConfigurationDefinition` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`) adds `audit.min_score` (0–100, default `null`), `audit.fail_on_incomplete` (default `false`), `audit.format` (any `--format` value, default `console`) and `audit.output` (default `null`), mapped by `BundleConfiguration` into `AuditExecutionConfiguration` and registered as container parameters; they work in the bundle's `symfony_security_auditor.yaml` and in the standalone binary's `config.yaml` and project `.symfony-security-auditor.yaml`, whose precedence the docs already state: flag, project file, user file, defaults. `AuditCommand` applies them through `AuditCommandInput::applyDefaults()` (`src/Command/`) before anything reads the input, so `--format console` wins over `audit.format: sarif` even though it names the default. A boolean flag cannot switch a configured `true` back off, so `--fail-on-incomplete` is now a negatable option (`?bool`) and `--no-fail-on-incomplete` is new, and a configured `audit.output` has `--no-output` beside `--output`: it prints the report for that run instead of writing it, `AuditCommandInput::applyDefaults()` leaves the configured path unapplied under it, and giving both flags is refused before the audit runs with `--output and --no-output cannot be combined: one writes the report to a file, the other prints it.` (`ConflictingCommandOptionsException::forOutputWithNoOutput()`). `audit.output` is a user-only key in the standalone binary (`StandaloneConfigLoader::USER_ONLY_PATHS`): a project `.symfony-security-auditor.yaml` ships with the audited repository, which must not choose where your report is written, and one declaring it is rejected before the run starts, naming the key. The keys are listed in `docs/versioning.md` and `resources/schema.json`. Tracked in [#408](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/408). A `--dry-run` never writes its zero-finding estimate to a configured `audit.output` (`AuditCommandInput::applyDefaults()`), which would have replaced the last real report; `--output` still writes one. A format given inside a cluster of flags (`-nf json`) is recognised as given (`GivenFormatOption`, `src/Command/GivenFormatOption.php`), where `hasParameterOption(['--format', '-f'])` missed it and let the configured format override the flag.

### Changed

- **`--path` now replaces `scan.included_paths` for the run: a command-line flag wins over the configuration.** `ProjectFileScanner::scan()` read only `scan.included_paths`, and `IngestionStage`, `ListScannedFilesUseCase` and `EstimateAuditCostUseCase` then narrowed its result to the `--path` values, so a path the configured scope did not reach listed nothing: `--path apps/api` on a monorepo with the default `included_paths` printed `No files matched`, whatever `docs/configuration.md` said about monorepos, and a project config in the working directory could silently narrow a run that `--path` was meant to widen. The new opt-in `ScopedProjectFileScannerInterface` (`src/Audit/Domain/Port/`) lets a scanner take the paths, `ProjectFileScanner::scanWithin()` scans exactly those directories or files under every other rule — file types, `scan.max_file_size_kb`, `scan.respect_gitignore`, secret scrubbing, the refusal of symlinks and of paths outside the project — and `ScopedScan` (`src/Audit/Application/Scan/`) picks the scan for the three callers. A custom scanner that does not implement the new interface keeps its result narrowed to the paths, as before. `--path .`, or the project root itself, still means the configured scope. The `--path` and `--show-scanned` help and the no-match warning (`Check that each --path exists in the project, relative to its root, and holds PHP, Twig, YAML or XML files.`) say so. **Upgrade note:** a configuration that narrows `scan.included_paths` (say to `src/Controller`) and a run with `--path src` used to audit `src/Controller` only and now audits all of `src`, so such a run reads more files and spends more; drop the `--path`, or give the narrower path, to keep the old scope. Tracked in [#407](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/407).

- **The standalone binary now reads `.symfony-security-auditor.yaml` from the project it audits, not from the folder it runs in.** `StandaloneApplicationFactory::projectConfigFile()` pointed the loader at the working directory (`$PWD`) when the application was built, before the command line was read, so `symfony-security-auditor audit ~/work/shop` run from any other folder skipped the `.symfony-security-auditor.yaml` of `~/work/shop` and layered the one of the folder it ran in: a gate or scan scope the repository pins applied only when you `cd`'d into it. `StandaloneApplication::projectPathGivenTo()` now reads the `project-path` the command line names by binding a copy of it to the audit command's own definition, which is the only way to tell that argument from the value of an option (`--path src /srv/app`), and `StandaloneApplicationFactory` hands the loader that project's file through `StandaloneConfigLoader::withProjectConfigFile()`. A relative `project-path` resolves against the working directory as the command does, and a command line that names no project, or does not bind, keeps the working directory's file. The file is held to the same guards as before (no `platform:`, `provider:` or `scan.import_sarif`, no user-only key, no loosened budget, plain text only) and the audit header still names it, so a repository can do no more through it than by being the folder you `cd` into. A relative `audit.baseline` in that file is read from the folder that holds it (`ProjectConfigPathAnchor`, `src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/ProjectConfigPathAnchor.php`), not from the working directory, which would otherwise have applied another folder's accepted findings to the project. `mcp:serve` still reads the working directory's file, since one server audits whichever project each tool call names. Refs [#407](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/407).

### Fixed

- **A model that answers with several tool calls in one turn no longer loses all but the first.** `PlatformResultExtractor::extractToolCalls()` read only the first `ToolCallResult` of a `MultiPartResult`, but the content-block bridges (Anthropic, Bedrock Claude, Gemini, Vertex) emit one part per tool call. With `audit.structured_collection` or `audit.reviewer_structured_collection` on, the default, the second and later `record_vulnerability` calls of a turn, and the later `record_review` verdicts of a batch, were never run, and the shortened answer was cached as the model's verdict. It now joins the tool calls of every part.
- **The advisory lookup no longer reads a hostile `composer.lock` whole, caches an output it cannot use, or keeps a failed or outdated answer.** `LockfileHashedAdvisoryCache` read `composer.lock` through `Filesystem::readFile()` with no limit, so a project whose `composer.lock` is a symlink to `/dev/zero` exhausted the audit host's memory; the new `LockfileHasher` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Advisory/`) hashes the lockfile for the cache and for `DeferredAdvisoryDatabase`, and a lockfile that is a symlink or larger than `LockfileHasher::MAX_LOCKFILE_BYTES` (8 MiB) now gets no hash, logs a warning and reaches `composer audit` uncached. The cache also stored whatever `composer audit` printed for 24 hours, so a PHP warning ahead of the JSON document switched `lookup_advisory` off for a day; it now stores only a payload `AdvisoryPayload` accepts (a JSON object holding an `advisories` map, the shape `ComposerAuditAdvisoryDatabase` reads). Last, `DeferredAdvisoryDatabase` memoized per project path alone and kept a failed load as an empty database, so one timeout or a missing `composer` silenced the lookup for the rest of the process and, in a long-lived `mcp:serve`, a `composer update` was never seen; it now memoizes per path and `composer.lock` hash and tries again after a load that `ComposerAuditAdvisoryDatabase::hasFailedToLoad()` reports as failed.
- **A standalone run no longer misreads a `%` in its cache or working directory as container syntax.** `StandaloneContainerFactory::create()` registered `kernel.cache_dir`, `kernel.build_dir` and `kernel.project_dir` unescaped, though they come from the user's home or XDG directories and `getcwd()`, so the container read any `%` pair in them as syntax: a cache directory `/tmp/%x%/cache` aborted the build with `The parameter "kernel.cache_dir" has a dependency on a non-existent parameter "x"`, `/tmp/a%%b/cache` silently became `/tmp/a%b/cache`, and `/tmp/%env(HOME)%/cache` was replaced by the value of the environment variable. The three parameters are now escaped with `ContainerParameterSyntax::escape()`, so the container reads back the path as written.
- **Overlapping scan paths no longer list a file twice.** `ProjectFileScanner` handed every configured directory to one `Finder`, so `scan.included_paths: ['src', 'src/Controller']` returned each file under `src/Controller` twice. Each file is now listed once, whatever the paths that reach it, and a directory listed inside another is no longer walked, read and secret-scrubbed a second time (`ProjectFileScanner::outermost()`).
- **Findings that collapse into one now keep the most severe, and a later verdict can no longer lower a severity already reported.** `AuditOrchestrator` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/AuditOrchestrator.php`) resolved every collapse in arrival order: `mergeRecoveredFindings()` let the last finding of an id win, `isSameIdDuplicate()` let a later validated verdict replace an earlier one whenever the severity differed — a `critical` verdict was replaced by a later `medium` one — and the overlap check dropped a finding at an overlapping range of the same file and type whatever its severity, so a `critical` reported after a `high` at lines 12-18 never reached the report. The new `FindingPrecedence` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/FindingPrecedence.php`) decides every collapse: a reviewer-validated finding beats any other, then the higher severity wins, then the higher confidence, and a tie keeps the finding seen first. A same-id verdict replaces an earlier validated one only when it raises the severity, or reclassifies the type at the same severity; a finding overlapping validated ones is admitted only when it outranks each of them, and then takes their place through the new `AuditContext::removeVulnerability()`. A rejected finding still never displaces a validated one, and the rejected verdicts the attacker is told about stay in the context. A replacement that differs from the finding it replaces by nothing but a higher confidence (`FindingPrecedence::onlyOutranksOnConfidence()`) swaps the stored finding but is not a new one, so a re-report with a slightly different confidence no longer sends the loop into another full attacker pass.
- **A project path that is not a directory, or an absolute `--path`, is now refused with the reason instead of ending as an empty scan.** `AuditCommand` printed its header, the warnings and `No files matched. Check your included_paths configuration and any --path filters.` before it noticed that the project is not a directory, so `audit src/Command` run from the wrong folder printed a warning and then `Project path "C:/Users/vince/src/Command" is not a valid directory`, and `audit --show-scanned` on a missing folder exited `0`. It now checks the directory right after the header and fails with exit code `1` and that error alone (`assertProjectIsDirectory()`, `src/Command/AuditCommand.php`). `--path "C:\Users\vince\demo\src\Command"` matched nothing: `ScanPathFilter` compares each `--path` as a prefix of a project-relative file path (`src/Command/Foo.php`), so an absolute path never matched and the run read as clean. `ScanPathResolver` (`src/Command/`) now makes an absolute path inside the project relative to its root, on every platform (a Windows drive letter and backslashes included, which Symfony's `Path` only reads on Windows itself), reads the project root itself as the whole project, and refuses one outside it with `The --path "…" lies outside the project "…". Give --path relative to the project root, for example --path src/Controller.` (`ScanPathOutsideProjectException`, exit code `1`). A scan that still lists nothing now says where it looked: `No files matched under "C:/Users/vince" for --path src/Command. Check that each --path is relative to the project root and that your included_paths configuration covers it.` (`AuditPresenterInterface::noFilesMatched()`, `@internal`).
- **A finding the model reported but the auditor could not read no longer turns its file into a clean one.** `VulnerabilityFactory` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/VulnerabilityFactory.php`) dropped every entry that failed validation or hydration — a `proof`, `remediation`, `attack_vector` or `vulnerable_code` over 5000 characters, a `line_end` below `line_start`, a `confidence` written as `85` for 85%, a `severity` or `type` in capitals — and `SequentialChunkAnalyzer` and `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer` then recorded the chunk `analyzed` and cached the raw answer anyway, so the loss was only counted in a log line and never retried: the file read as clean on that run and on every cached run after it. `VulnerabilityFactory` now recovers what can be recovered safely: it cuts a free-text field over its limit to the limit (`title` 500 characters; `description`, `vulnerable_code`, `attack_vector`, `proof` and `remediation` 5000), swaps an inverted line range (a `line_end` below 1 reads as `line_start`), lower-cases and trims `type` and `severity`, and scales a `confidence` above 1 and up to 100 down to the 0–1 range. A new `ChunkOutcomeRecorder` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Chunk/ChunkOutcomeRecorder.php`) then settles each answered chunk for both analyzers and both collection modes: when an entry is still dropped (a blank or over-long `file_path`, an unknown type, a bare string among the findings) the chunk is recorded `errored` and left out of the attacker cache, with the warning `Attacker answer held entries that could not be read as findings; the chunk is recorded as errored and left out of the cache` naming the files and the drop reasons. The report is then incomplete, so `--fail-on-incomplete` can fire, and the next run asks the model again; the findings that did read are kept. A missing `confidence` is not guessed at: it still reads as `0.5`, under the `0.6` floor `AuditOrchestrator` applies.
- **`audit:run --min-score` refuses a value outside 0 to 100 instead of failing every run or gating none.** The gate compares `--min-score` with the normalized score (`AuditReport::normalizedScore()`, which spans 0 to 100), but `AuditCommandInput::$minScore` (`src/Command/AuditCommandInput.php`) accepted any integer: `--min-score=150` made every run exit `1` with `Audit failed a configured gate. Risk: SAFE. Score: 100/100.`, and `--min-score=-1` silently disabled the gate. `AuditCommandInput::assertMinScoreInRange()` now throws `InvalidMinScoreException` (`src/Command/Exception/InvalidMinScoreException.php`) for such a value, which `AuditCommand` reports as `--min-score must be between 0 and 100, got 150.` with exit code `1` — the code a conflicting option already gets — before the output files are checked and before the preview or the audit spends anything. `0` and `100` stay valid, and omitting the option still gates on the risk level alone.
- **A cheap finding the deep pass judged and dropped no longer comes back into the report when escalation is on.** `EscalatingAttackerAgent` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/EscalatingAttackerAgent.php`) drops a cheap-model finding on a file the deep pass analyzed and did not re-report, since its silence is a discard. But the cheap pass had already recorded that finding through `CoverageRecorderInterface::recordFoundVulnerability()` (`SequentialChunkAnalyzer` and `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer` record every chunk's findings so a later abort can recover them), and `AuditOrchestrator::analyzeWithRecovery()` merges everything `drainFoundVulnerabilities()` returns into the attacker's result on a normal return too — it recovers what a chunk recorded and then failed to return — so the dropped finding was re-added, sent to the reviewer and could reach the report. After judging, the agent now withdraws the findings it discarded from the recorder's recoverable findings (`withdrawFromRecovery()`), leaving what it kept and what a chunk recorded but could not return; the abort path still recovers the cheap findings when the deep pass aborts.
- **`init` refuses a provider instance named by an environment placeholder instead of writing a configuration the next run cannot select.** `InitRefusal::forNamedInstance()` tested the instance name with `ContainerParameterSyntax::isAbsentFrom()`, which deliberately lets a whole-value `%env(VAR)%` through for a model or a URL, so `init --provider='generic.%env(X)%'` exited `0` after writing `provider: generic.%env(X)%` and a platform key `'%env(X)%'`, and installing the bridge. An instance name is a key, never a value to substitute, so it is now refused with exit code `2` whenever `ContainerParameterSyntax::holdsReference()` finds a `%...%` pair in it, a whole-value placeholder included; `init` writes nothing and installs no bridge. The model and base URL checks are unchanged.
- **A scan that found no file is no longer presented as SAFE, grade A in the reports and the command output.** `audit:run` already exited `1` for a run whose scan discovered no file (a mistyped `project-path`, a `scan.included_paths` entry matching nothing), but the verdict stayed with `AuditExitCodeResolver` (`src/Command/AuditExitCodeResolver.php`) and `AuditTool` (`src/Command/Mcp/AuditTool.php`): `AuditReport::hasNoVerdict()` (`src/Audit/Domain/Model/AuditReport.php`) only knew a run that analyzed none of its files, so the rest read the empty report as clean. `AuditPresenter::result()` (`src/Command/AuditPresenter.php`) printed `Audit failed a configured gate. Risk: SAFE. Score: 100/100. 0 vulnerabilities found.` under exit `1`, the console, executive, Markdown and HTML reports headlined `SAFE` with `No validated vulnerabilities found.`, the GitHub comment headlined `Security audit: A (100/100)`, and SARIF recorded `executionSuccessful: true`. `AuditReport::hasNoVerdict()` is now also true when the scan discovered no file, which `AuditExitCodeResolver::hasNoVerdict()` simply delegates to, and `IncompleteAuditNotice` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Report/IncompleteAuditNotice.php`) states `Audit incomplete: the scan found no file to audit, so this report has no verdict and cannot vouch that the project is free of vulnerabilities.` for it. Every human format now reads `UNKNOWN (no file was analyzed)` with that notice and no grade, SARIF sets `executionSuccessful: false` with it as a tool execution notification, the JUnit report adds its errored `Audit completeness` test case, the GitHub annotations report its warning, and the command prints `The scan found no file to audit, so the run has no verdict. A run with no verdict cannot pass, so it fails.` on stderr when the report goes to stdout. The JSON report keeps its field values (`complete`, `risk_level`, `score`, `grade`), as it does for a run that analyzed no file. A `--since` run whose diff left nothing changed still has a verdict.
- **An `access_control` path that is not a valid regular expression no longer aborts the prompt build.** `AccessControlRuleMatcher::rolesForPath()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Prompt/`) ran `preg_match()` on each `access_control` path read from the audited repository's `security.yaml` without checking it compiles, so `path: ^/(admin` raised `preg_match(): Compilation failed: missing closing parenthesis at offset 8` as an `E_WARNING`; under a warning-throwing error handler (Symfony debug, PHPUnit `failOnWarning`, a converting handler in `mcp:serve`) the warning became an exception and the run stopped before any chunk was sent. The match now captures that warning, restores the previous handler and treats the pattern as matching nothing, so the route is tagged `LACKS_ACCESS_CHECK` unless a later valid rule covers it.
- **A chunk that explored up to `audit.max_tool_iterations` now records what it holds and completes, instead of failing on every run.** `SequentialToolLoop` and `ToolConversationWavefront` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/LLM/`) ended the conversation at the cap with a `max_tool_iterations` answer, so a chunk the model was still reading for was recorded as `errored` and left out of the cache: on a project of about a hundred files a default run ended `Audit incomplete` and paid for the same chunks again every time, although `docs/configuration.md` promised the attacker is "forced to commit to a final answer". The tool result that precedes the last round a conversation is allowed now carries a notice (`FinalRound::NOTICE`) telling the model that round is its last and nothing it reads after it will reach it — appended to the result rather than sent as a message of its own, because a provider may refuse a user turn right after a tool result — and a last round that calls a tool marked `Tool\RecordingToolInterface` (`RecordVulnerabilityTool`, `RecordReviewTool`) concludes the conversation as an `end_turn` answer, with the findings it recorded; a last round that answers in text is read as it always was. Nothing else changes the request — the tool list is the one the first round sent, so a provider's prompt cache is kept, and no round is added, so the cost of a chunk is bounded by the cap as before; a cap of `1` leaves no tool result to carry the notice, but a first round that records still concludes. A model that ignores the notice and asks to read one more file still ends as `max_tool_iterations`. The cap stays part of the attacker cache key (`ContainerParameterRegistrar::attackerToolsSalt()`), so a chunk finished this way is cached under the cap that cut its reading short and runs again when the cap is raised. The docs say what happens at the cap, and `docs/extending.md` tells a custom `RecordVulnerabilityToolFactoryInterface` to mark its tool. Closes [#406](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/406).
- **A chunk that fails now says why on its progress line.** `✗ chunk 6/12 failed (26s)` named no cause: the reason went only to the application log, which the standalone binary never writes (`StandaloneContainerFactory` registers a `NullLogger`), so `-v` and `-vv` showed nothing there and `docs/troubleshooting.md` still told every user to re-run with them. `SequentialChunkAnalyzer` and `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Chunk/`) now record why each chunk ended errored, through the new opt-in `FailureReasonRecorderInterface` (`src/Audit/Domain/Pipeline/`, `@internal`, beside `RejectedFindingRecorderInterface`) that `StatusTrackingCoverageRecorder` keeps per file, worded by `ChunkFailureReason` and carried as `reason` next to `status: errored` in the `attacker.chunk.completed` context that `ChunkCompletionContext` builds. `ConsoleProgressReporter` and `PlainProgressReporter` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Progress/`) print it after the duration: `✗ chunk 6/12 failed (26s) — tool-call limit reached (audit.max_tool_iterations)`. The other reasons are `output token limit reached (max_output_tokens)`, `answer withheld by the provider content filter`, `the model returned no content`, `the answer was not valid JSON`, `the file is too large for the model input limit`, and, for any other failure of the call, its message on one line cut at 120 characters. The text is collapsed to one line, stripped of control characters and escaped like any other LLM-sourced text, and a chunk that was analyzed carries no `reason`. `docs/troubleshooting.md` explains each reason under `✗ chunk N/M failed` and now says that `-v` only adds to the log of a Symfony application.
- **A finding is named by the file it is about, however the model spelled the path, so the reviewer sees its code and one weakness is one finding.** `Vulnerability::filePath()` is free text the model echoes back, and only a leading `./` was ever dropped, and only when comparing: a finding reported as `/abs/project/src/A.php`, `/src/A.php` or `src\A.php` kept that spelling, so `CodeContextResolver::resolve()` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Review/CodeContextResolver.php`) found no file and the reviewer judged the finding without its code — it rejected it and the rejection was cached — and two spellings of one file gave two ids and two fingerprints, so the same weakness was reported twice. `VulnerabilityFactory::fromList()` now takes the files the findings were reported on and `EchoedFilePath::resolve()` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/EchoedFilePath.php`) names each finding by the relative path of the file its path denotes — compared on forward slashes without a leading `./` or `/`, against both the relative and the absolute path of the chunk's files. `SequentialChunkAnalyzer`, `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer` and `AttackerChunkCache` pass the chunk to every hydration, a cache hit and a finding recovered after an abort included. A path that denotes no file of the chunk stays as the model gave it.
- **A hyphenated `offline-only` or provider instance name in `config.yaml` is now read the way the audit reads it.** `StandaloneApplicationFactory::offlineOnly()` and `ConfiguredCredentialVariable::parsedConfig()` parsed the user config raw with `Yaml::parseFile()`, so they missed the hyphen-to-underscore folding that `StandaloneConfigFileReader` (and `symfony/config`) applies. With `privacy: {offline-only: true}` the audit enforced the offline guard while `self-update` still reached `models.dev` through `ModelsDevCatalogRefresher` instead of `NullPricingCatalogRefresher`; with `provider: generic.my_gw` and a `platform.generic.my-gw` block, `auth:set` and `auth:status` claimed no credential variable was configured although the run worked. Both now read the file through `StandaloneConfigFileReader`, failing closed (offline) or naming no variable on a file it refuses as malformed or oversized, as they did on a YAML parse error.
- **A SARIF report no longer marks as accepted more findings than the baseline accepts.** `BaselineProcessor` credits an accepted baseline entry once — one entry accepts one finding, so a second finding with the same fingerprint (same type, file and title, at another line) stays in the report and trips the gate — but `SarifReportRenderer::resultFor()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Report/SarifReportRenderer.php`) tested each finding with `in_array()` against the baselined fingerprints, so every finding sharing a listed fingerprint carried the `suppressions` entry and Code Scanning showed a new finding as accepted while `audit:run` exited `1` for it. `SarifReportRenderer::renderWithSuppressions()` now asks `AuditReport::withoutFingerprints()` — the count-aware filter the baseline applies to the other formats — which findings stay standing, and suppresses the others, so the most severe finding of a shared fingerprint takes the credit first in both.
- **A `record_vulnerability` or `record_review` call missing a required key is no longer acknowledged as recorded.** `RecordVulnerabilityTool::execute()` and `RecordReviewTool::execute()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Tool/`) trusted the platform to enforce the tool's input schema and answered `recorded` to whatever arguments they received, so on a provider that does not enforce it a finding without a `file_path`, or a verdict without an `id` or `accepted`, was accepted, dropped later at hydration, and the model never learned to fix the call. They now check the call against the `required` list of their own schema through `RequiredArguments`, so the two cannot drift: a missing (or `null`) argument is answered with `Error: missing required argument "file_path".` and a non-string value for a string argument with `Error: argument "id" must be a string.`, the model retries from inside the tool loop, and nothing reaches the collector. The success answer `recorded` is unchanged, and a number or boolean a provider stringified is still accepted, since `VulnerabilityFactory` and `VerdictApplier` normalise those downstream.

### Security

- **`self-update` no longer targets a same-named executable in the current folder when the binary was started by a bare name.** On a platform without `/proc/self/exe` (macOS), `RunningBinaryLocator` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/SelfUpdate/RunningBinaryLocator.php`) resolved the entry path with `realpath()`, which reads a bare `symfony-security-auditor` against the working directory before `PATH` was ever consulted, so running `self-update` from a folder holding an executable file of that name staged the download next to it and renamed it over that file instead of the installed binary. A bare name is now resolved through `PATH` only, as the shell does, and a name that carries a directory is resolved against the working directory only, so it is no longer searched for again under each `PATH` entry; when `PATH` holds no such executable, `self-update` fails with `Could not determine the path of the running binary to replace; self-update is only supported for the standalone binary.`
- **The MCP `audit` tool no longer runs a cost-budgeted audit on a model it cannot price.** `AuditCommand` asks `UnpricedModelBudgetGuard::permitsRun()` before it spends anything and, under `--no-interaction`, refuses a run whose `audit.budget.max_cost_usd` cannot be enforced because a configured model has no published price (a typo, an unlisted model); `AuditTool` (`src/Command/Mcp/AuditTool.php`) never asked, so over `mcp:serve` — in the bundle and the standalone binary — that cap never tripped and the real spend was unbounded. `AuditTool` now takes the guard and calls its new `assertBudgetEnforceable()` before the audit, which throws `UnenforceableBudgetException` when a cost budget is set and a configured model is unpriced; the tool answers it as a `ToolCallException` carrying the sentence `audit:run` prints (`Refusing to start a budgeted audit with an unpriceable model in non-interactive mode. Configure a model with published pricing, or remove audit.budget.max_cost_usd.`), which now ends with the names of the unpriced models (`Unpriced model(s): mystery-model.`) on the command's line as well. A run with no cost budget, or whose models are all priced, is unchanged.
- **`self-update` pins every request to HTTPS with TLS 1.2 or newer and bounds what it downloads.** `ProcessReleaseClient::defaultProcessBuilder()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/SelfUpdate/ProcessReleaseClient.php`) ran `curl -fsSL` with no protocol or TLS restriction and no size limit: `-L` followed a redirect to plain `http://` (or to anything curl speaks), a server could negotiate an older TLS version, and a response had no size bound beyond the transfer time. Every call now passes `--proto =https`, which also refuses a redirect off HTTPS, and `--tlsv1.2`; `get()` — the release JSON and the `.sha256` line — adds `--max-filesize 1048576` (1 MiB) and `download()` — the binary and the pricing catalog — adds `--max-filesize 268435456` (256 MiB). A response over the limit, or a redirect to another protocol, fails with `Failed to download "<url>".` and nothing is replaced.
- **A pricing catalog with a negative, infinite or input-less price no longer bills a model at a rate nobody set, and a refreshed catalog the run cannot use no longer switches pricing off.** `ModelsDevPricingProvider` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Pricing/`) read every `cost` it found as written: a negative or infinite rate (`1e999` decodes to `INF`) priced a call below zero or past any budget, and a cost with no `input` rate priced the model at `$0` while `hasModel()` answered `true`, so the unpriced-model budget guard never fired for it. A refreshed catalog the provider could not read, decode or price a model from shadowed the packaged one and left every model unpriced (`[]`), which switched budget enforcement off for the run. `ModelsDevCatalog` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Pricing/`) now defines a priced cost: an `input` rate, plus any numeric `output`, `cache_read` and `cache_write` rate, that are finite and not negative. The provider treats any other cost as an unpriced model, so another provider's valid listing of the same id can still price it. `ModelsDevCatalogRefresher` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/SelfUpdate/`) drops such a `cost` from the catalog it installs and refuses a download left with no priced model. When the refreshed catalog is unusable, the provider falls back to the packaged one and logs `Ignoring the unusable pricing catalog override; falling back to the packaged catalog` with the reason and the path, and `doctor` names the catalog the run actually prices from.
- **The HTML report no longer carries raw terminal escape sequences from a finding.** `HtmlReportRenderer::escape()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Report/HtmlReportRenderer.php`) stripped only the Unicode bidi overrides, so an ESC byte in a model-written field (`\x1b]0;…\x07`, `\x1b[2J`) reached the saved file untouched and acted on a terminal the moment the report was printed with `cat`. It now strips what the console, Markdown, JUnit and annotation renderers already strip, through `TerminalTextSanitizer::stripControlCharacters()`: the C0 and C1 control characters and the bidi overrides, keeping the line breaks and tabs of a code snippet.
- **A bare URL or `www.` address in a finding no longer renders as a live link in the Markdown and GitHub comment reports.** `MarkdownTextEscaper::fences()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Report/MarkdownTextEscaper.php`) escaped `[`, `]`, `<`, `>` and `@`, but GFM links a bare `https://…`, `http://…`, `ftp://…` or `www.…` on its own, so a finding title, description or model name carrying one rendered a clickable attacker link in a report a reviewer trusts, and `[click](https://evil.example)` was left with a live `https://evil.example` inside its escaped brackets. The colon of `://` and the dot after `www` are now entity-encoded (`https&#58;//evil.example`, `www&#46;evil.example`) the way `@` already is, which also defuses an email address: the text reads as written and no GFM autolink matches it.
- **`audit:baseline --annotate` shows a finding's title and file as text, not as console markup.** The reason prompt (`BaselineCommand::reasons()`, `src/Command/BaselineCommand.php`) printed the title and file of each new finding from the report into a line Symfony Console formats, so a title such as `<error>Boom</error> <href=https://evil.example>click</>` was shown as `Boom click` (the tags consumed, the link attached in a terminal that supports hyperlinks) and `<fg=red>` restyled the prompt. Both values now go through `OutputFormatter::escape()` after the control-character strip, so the prompt shows them exactly as written.

## [1.21.0] — 2026-10-04 — Gateway

A release about reaching the model you actually run, and never mistaking a partial audit for a clean one. `init` now configures any OpenAI-compatible AI gateway, a local Ollama, AWS Bedrock and the new platforms of `symfony/ai` 0.14, keeps the API key for you (`auth:set`), and installs the bridge even for a platform whose block you finish by hand; the standalone binary also serves the auditor over MCP. A run that could not analyze every file now says so in every report format and in the GitHub Action, `--fail-on-incomplete` turns that into a failing exit code, a run that analyzed nothing has no verdict, and an answer cut short, a connection cut off mid-response or a chunk too large for the model is recovered instead of ending the audit. The audited repository's own configuration can no longer weaken the run, and the secret scrubber redacts more credential shapes while no longer hiding the code an audit has to read.

### Added

- **`--fail-on-incomplete` fails a run that could not analyze every file, and such a run now always says so.** An audit that ran to its end with some file not fully analyzed — a scan or LLM call failed for it — exited with the code its gates earned and said nothing about it, so a partial report passed CI. `audit:run --fail-on-incomplete` now exits with the new code `3` for such a run (a tripped `--fail-on` or `--min-score` gate still exits `1`, as does a run with no verdict — see "A report never calls an aborted or partly failed audit clean" under Fixed — and an aborted run keeps `1` or `2`), including under `--generate-baseline`. Without the option, the run prints an `Audit incomplete: N file(s) could not be fully analyzed …` warning — naming the option when no gate failed the run — on stderr when the report goes to stdout (`AuditPresenter::incompleteRunNotice()`). The GitHub Action gains a `fail-on-incomplete` input, a `complete` output read from a JSON or SARIF report, and a warning annotation when the report is incomplete, the input is off and the run exited `0`.

- **A call is billed as the model the provider says answered it.** A gateway routing on its own (`openrouter/auto`), a failover platform or an alias the provider resolves (`claude-opus-4-8` answered as a dated release) used to be billed at the configured model's rate whatever actually ran. `symfony/ai` 0.14 reports the serving model in `TokenUsage::getModel()`; `PlatformResultExtractor::extractReportedModel()` reads it, every per-call `LLMResponse` carries it through the new `LLMResponse::withReportedModel()` / `LLMResponse::reportedModel()`, and `BudgetTracker` prices the call through `CostCalculator::billedModel()`, which takes the reported model when the serving platform's own listing prices it — any listing when that platform has none, through the new opt-in `ServingPlatformPricingProviderInterface` port that `ModelsDevPricingProvider` implements — and keeps the configured one otherwise, so another provider's rate for the same id never sets the bill and a gateway answering under an id no catalog knows never drops the cost to `$0`. The budget guard and `estimated_cost_usd` follow; `by_model` stays keyed by the configured model and names the models its calls were billed as under the additive `billed_models` key.

- **`init` configures AWS Bedrock.** Bedrock's default InvokeModel route needs an AWS SDK client service, which a standalone config cannot declare. `symfony/ai` 0.14 adds the Bedrock Mantle routes, which take an API key, so `init --provider=bedrock.<instance>` now writes a block that boots: `BedrockMantleRoute` picks `api: messages` for an `anthropic.*` model, `api: responses` for `google.gemma-*` and `api: completions` for every other one, and `init` also installs the `generic` or `openresponses` bridge those two routes need, in the same `composer require` as `symfony/ai-bedrock-platform` (`BridgeInstallerInterface::install()` takes further providers). The key is read from `BEDROCK_API_KEY`; `--no-api-key`, which accepts `bedrock`, leaves it out so requests are signed with AWS SigV4. A model that does not name its vendor, such as the default `claude-opus-4-8`, is refused with exit code `2` and an example of the id Bedrock expects.

- **The standalone binary serves the auditor over MCP too.** `symfony-security-auditor mcp:serve` starts the same Model Context Protocol server `bin/console mcp:serve` already offered in bundle mode, so Claude Code, Claude Desktop, Cursor, VS Code, Windsurf, Gemini CLI, Codex CLI or any other MCP client can run an audit without a Symfony application around it. `StandaloneApplicationFactory` registers it lazily beside `audit:run`, built from the user-level `config.yaml` by `StandaloneConsoleCommandFactory::createMcpServer()`; like a real audit it refuses to start without the provider credential the configuration names. The `mcp:serve` docs and the README now show how to register it with each client, and say plainly that the audit runs on the configured provider rather than on the assistant's model, so an API key is still needed unless the platform is a local one such as Ollama.

- **`init` installs the bridge for a platform it cannot configure and prints the block to finish by hand, instead of writing one the container refuses.** `init` only ever wrote an `api_key`, so for a platform that rejects one or requires a field `init` never asks for, it wrote a file the next run could not boot, for example:

  ```text
  The child config "version" under "ai.platform.cartesia" must be configured.
  ```

  `HandWrittenPlatforms` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`) names the six such platforms and what each needs: `azure` (a `deployment`), `cartesia` (a `version`), `higgsfield` (an `api_secret`), and `dockermodelrunner`, `lmstudio` and `transformersphp` (no `api_key` node at all). For these, `init` installs the bridge the same way it does for every other platform — a hand-run `composer require` would miss the PHP version pin the binary needs — then prints a ready-to-edit `config.yaml` block (`HandWrittenPlatformBlock`) with a `<placeholder>` for every value only you know, and exits `2` without writing a configuration. Before installing anything it checks what it is given the way the written path does: an instance name the config file could not be read back with, the container would read as a parameter or no service can be named after (`azure.0`, `azure.%gw%`, `azure.o'brien`), an instance on a platform taking a single block (`lmstudio.x`) and an `--env-var` that is no variable name are refused with exit `2`, so none of them reaches the printed block or a bridge install. `cache` and `failover` get neither: `symfony/ai-bundle` wires `failover` to a rate limiter service and `cache` to the serializer and the Symfony clock, which the standalone container does not define and `config.yaml` cannot declare, so no standalone block for them could boot. `init` refuses both with exit `2`, installs no bridge, prints no block and says why: "… wraps other platforms through a rate limiter service, which only a Symfony application defines …". They keep working from the bundle. `bedrock` is written with a Mantle route (see "`init` configures AWS Bedrock"). `doctor` now points at `init --provider=<platform>` for a missing bridge. Closes [#370](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/370).

- **`init` can now configure a local Ollama end to end, and writes no credential for a platform that authenticates nobody.** `init --provider=ollama` exited `0` and reported success on a configuration the next run could not use: it wrote `api_key: '%env(OLLAMA_API_KEY)%'` for the one platform `docs/configuration.md` lists as needing no credential, and omitted the `endpoint` the bridge has no default for, without which a request has no base URI to go to. The next command stopped at:

  ```text
  The environment variable "OLLAMA_API_KEY", referenced by your config, is not set.
  ```

  Two options close it. **`--endpoint`** writes the connection URL for the platforms that name it that way — `deepgram`, `elevenlabs`, `minimax`, `ollama`, `together` and `venice` — as `--base-url` does for those naming it `base_url`, and `init` asks for it on those platforms, an empty answer keeping the platform's default; a platform declares one spelling or the other, never both, so each option rejects what the other accepts and names the one that platform takes instead (`--base-url` on `ollama` answers `use --endpoint`). **`--no-api-key`** writes no `api_key` at all, for the platforms whose key `symfony/ai-bundle` leaves optional (`bedrock`, `deepgram`, `elevenlabs`, `generic`, `ollama`, `openresponses`); it is refused for every other platform, whose container validation fails without one — `vertexai` included: its `api_key` node is optional in the schema, but a validation closure requires it unless `location` and `project_id` are set, which `init` never writes. The knowledge test also reads the bundle's validation closures, so a platform of that kind cannot slip into the list.

  `ollama` needs neither flag to come out right. It is the only one of those platforms with nowhere to send a request without its endpoint: its `endpoint` node declares no default, and while `together`'s declares none either, that bridge falls back to `https://api.together.xyz`. So `init` now treats it as one you host yourself: the endpoint is required, and no credential is invented unless `--env-var` names one, which is what Ollama Cloud wants. Asked for no endpoint, `init` refuses with exit code `2` and writes nothing, rather than reporting success on a file that cannot run. The keyless shape is also what `privacy.offline_only: true` has always expected, so the pairing the docs recommend is reachable through `init` for the first time.

  `EndpointPlatforms` and `OptionalApiKeyPlatforms` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`) restate what the bundle declares, and `PlatformShapeKnowledgeTest` reads its `config/platform/*.php` back so an upstream change fails the build instead of leaving the lists quietly wrong. `WrittenConfigurationBootsEndToEndTest` now boots the keyless local-Ollama block through the real loader and compiled container. Closes [#369](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/369).

- **The standalone binary can now hold your API key for you, so a machine is set up once instead of every shell.** `init` (`src/Command/InitCommand.php`) only ever asked _which environment variable_ holds the key and wrote `%env(ANTHROPIC_API_KEY)%` into the config — it never asked for the key itself, so the guided `curl … | SSA_INIT=1 sh` install ended pointing at a variable nobody had set, and the first audit failed with:

  ```text
  The environment variable "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", referenced by your config, is not set.
  ```

  `init` now finishes by asking for the key (hidden input, skippable with Enter), and three commands manage it afterwards: **`auth:set`** stores or replaces it, **`auth:status`** reports which key an audit would use and where it came from, and **`auth:remove`** forgets it. `FilesystemCredentialStore` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`) keeps it in `credentials.json` beside the config file, keyed by the variable the config names — so switching provider cannot make a run pick up the previous provider's key.

  **An exported variable still wins**, ahead of anything stored, so Docker, Kubernetes, CI and a `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=$(pass show …) audit .` prefix are unaffected; `auth:status` warns when an export is shadowing a stored key. A container with no resolvable home directory simply has nothing stored and falls back to the environment exactly as before. See [Providing the API key](docs/configuration.md#providing-the-api-key).

  **The key is stored owner-only, and an exposed one is refused rather than used.** The store tightens its directory to `0700`, creates `credentials.json` empty and tightens it to `0600`, and only then writes the key into it — a directory whose mode cannot be tightened stops the write before any file is left in it — so the secret never occupies a path the process umask has left group- or world-readable. On a read, a file others can open stops the run:

  ```text
  The stored credentials at "…/credentials.json" are readable by other users on this machine (permissions 0644). Anyone who could read them may already have your API key, so rotate it with your provider, then run "chmod 600 '…'".
  ```

  The path in that command is quoted for a POSIX shell, so it pastes as is even when it holds spaces or quotes. Only reading refuses: `auth:set` and `auth:remove` rewrite the file and restore `0600` as they go, so an exposed key is always replaceable or deletable from the tool itself rather than only by hand. Windows has no POSIX permission bits, so the check is skipped there and the file is protected by the user-profile ACL it inherits from `%APPDATA%` — documented as the weaker guarantee it is, rather than claimed as parity.

  **The key is named in output, never printed.** `CredentialIdentity` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/CredentialIdentity.php`) renders a credential as a masked preview (`sk-ant…qF4A` — first six and last four characters, matching what a provider console shows) plus a truncated SHA-256 fingerprint (`SHA256:ed9ff73cc4b2cd57`) that identifies it exactly while revealing nothing. Anything shorter than 24 characters, or carrying bytes outside printable ASCII, is masked entirely. Every audit run prints the preview in its header; `auth:status` and `doctor` print both.

  `auth:set` checks it can store the key before asking for it, and with no per-user configuration directory refuses up front with "No per-user configuration directory could be resolved, so there is nowhere to store the credentials…". The prompt it shares with `init` (`HiddenCredentialPrompt`, `src/Command/`) never falls back to echoing: a terminal that cannot hide input is told to export the variable instead (`auth:set` exits `2`, `init` still writes its configuration), and stdin closing mid-prompt counts as nothing entered. The three commands refuse, with exit code `2`, a variable name no `%env()%` placeholder could read back (`EnvironmentVariableName`, `src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`), which `FilesystemCredentialStore::write()` refuses too, so the store never writes a file it cannot encode, and `auth:set` and `auth:remove` report a store they cannot use with its own error and exit code `1`. With several platforms configured, `auth:set`, `auth:status`, the audit header and `doctor` describe the key of the selected `provider:` (`PlatformApiKey::valueForProvider()`); the whole `platform:` block is searched only when no provider is selected. A `credentials.json` that no longer parses is replaced by the next `auth:set`; reading it and `auth:remove` still refuse it, and a file that cannot be read at all still fails a write.

  `auth:status` checks the environment first, so an exported key is reported even when `credentials.json` cannot be read — that is only a warning — and it reads a `%env(file:VAR)%` key through its file, also when `--env-var` names that variable: the file's content is described as the key (`Source: the file VAR names`), and a file that cannot be read or is empty exits `1` with the reason. Only a platform's `api_key` falls back to the store: an unset variable behind a `base_url` or `endpoint` is reported as that variable, never as a missing API key — `doctor` lists it under `Configuration`, not `API key` (`MissingEnvironmentVariableException::$concernsCredential`) — and a `--dry-run` never opens the credential file.

- **The standalone configuration can now take the provider credential from a file.** `StandalonePlatformConfigResolver` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/StandalonePlatformConfigResolver.php`) matched `%env(VAR)%` and nothing else, so an environment variable holding the key itself was the only way into a standalone run, and `%env(file:VAR)%` — the Symfony processor syntax bundle users already know — resolved to a lookup for a variable literally named `file:VAR`, failing with:

  ```text
  The environment variable "file:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY_FILE", referenced by your config, is not set.
  ```

  It now reads the file whose path `VAR` holds and strips surrounding whitespace, so Docker and Kubernetes secrets, `systemd` `LoadCredential=` and a plain `0600` file all work without a shell being involved. An unreadable file or a file holding only whitespace stops the run before the provider is contacted (`UnreadableCredentialFileException`, which names the variable and shows its value only as the masked preview a key gets, so a key put behind `file:` by mistake is never printed), and an unset variable falls through to the ordinary `MissingEnvironmentVariableException`; `doctor` reports it under its `API key` check, and `--dry-run` tolerates all three because it never reaches the provider. See [Providing the API key](docs/configuration.md#providing-the-api-key).

- **The audit header names a project config layered over the user config.** When a `.symfony-security-auditor.yaml` in the working directory tunes the run (paths, profile, models, a tighter budget), `StandaloneContainerFactory` (`src/Standalone/StandaloneContainerFactory.php`) now adds a pre-flight notice naming the file, so a setting coming from the audited checkout rather than from the user's own config is never silent.

- **An AI gateway behind a custom URL and token can now be configured.** `symfony/ai-generic-platform` is the `symfony/ai` bridge for an arbitrary OpenAI-compatible endpoint behind a `base_url` plus an `api_key`, which is the shape of every corporate AI gateway, yet it was absent from both platform tables, from `composer.json` `suggest` and from every documented example. Finding it was not enough either, because it is declared with `useAttributeAsKey` and so registers as `ai.platform.generic.<instance>`, while `StandaloneConfigFactory` wrote a flat `platform: {<provider>: {api_key: …}}` and `StandaloneContainerFactory::selectActivePlatform()` looked up `ai.platform.<provider>`. `init --provider=generic` therefore produced a config the container rejected with:

  ```text
  Invalid type for path "ai.platform.generic.api_key". Expected "array", but got "string"
  ```

  and repairing it by hand produced:

  ```text
  The selected provider "generic" is not present in the "platform:" block of your config.
  ```

  which named the one key that was plainly present. `StandaloneConfigFactory` now nests the connection under its instance, so `provider: generic.my_gateway` selects the `my_gateway` instance of the `generic` platform, and `init --provider=generic.<instance>` writes that block with its base URL (see "`init --base-url`"). The nesting covers all six instance-keyed platforms (`InstanceKeyedPlatforms`, `src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`); `init` writes a bootable block for the two whose prototype is `base_url` plus `api_key`, namely `generic` and `openresponses`, while `azure` also requires a `deployment` and gets a block to finish by hand, `bedrock` is written with a Mantle route, and `cache` and `failover` are refused. A provider naming an instance-keyed platform alone (`--provider=generic`), or an instance of a platform that takes a single block (`--provider=anthropic.prod`), is refused with exit code `2` and the shape to use instead. Only the platform half of the provider is lowercased and both halves are trimmed (`ProviderKeyNormalizer`, `ProviderKey::of()`), so the instance keeps the name you typed, except that a hyphenated one is folded the way `symfony/config` folds it: `generic.my-gateway` is written `my_gateway` in both places (`ConfigKeyInstanceName`). `init` refuses, with exit `2` and nothing written, an instance name the config file could not be read back with (`0`, `.inf`, `.nan`, a YAML tag such as `!php/const`, the merge key `<<`), one no service id can hold (a single quote, a NUL, a carriage return, a newline or a trailing backslash — `PlatformServiceId`), one holding a `%...%` pair the container would read as a parameter (`ContainerParameterSyntax`), and a provider naming no platform before the dot (`.anthropic`); any other number is accepted, so `generic.-1` is written `generic._1`.

  At run time a bare `provider: generic` reports that the platform is configured per instance and lists the instances it found, and a mistyped instance names the real ones: `The "generic" platform has no "typo" instance. Configured instances: eu.` `symfony/ai-generic-platform`, `symfony/ai-albert-platform` and `symfony/ai-amazee-ai-platform` join the README platform table and `composer.json` `suggest`, and a new [Instance-keyed platforms](docs/configuration.md#instance-keyed-platforms) section documents the nested `platform:` block, the compound `provider:` selector, and that `base_url` is the origin only: the `generic` bridge appends its own `completions_path` (default `/v1/chat/completions`), so a `base_url` ending in `/v1` would request `/v1/v1/chat/completions`. Refs [#365](https://github.com/vinceAmstoutz/symfony-security-auditor/issues/365).

- **`init --base-url`, and a base-URL prompt, for the platforms that require one.** `albert` and `amazeeai` declare `base_url` as a required child of a flat block, and `generic` and `openresponses` of every instance, but `init` only ever wrote an `api_key`, so `init --provider=albert` reported success on a configuration the next run aborted with:

  ```text
  The child config "base_url" under "ai.platform.albert" must be configured.
  ```

  `init` now asks for the base URL on those four platforms (`BaseUrlPlatforms::writableNames()`, `src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`), in a prompt that names the platform and offers no default, and `--base-url` supplies it without the prompt. An empty answer, `--base-url=` or a whitespace-only value is refused with exit code `2` and nothing written, and so is a prompt that reaches end of input — a `RUN` line or a CI step that forgot `--no-interaction` — rather than aborting with exit `1`. `azure` and `higgsfield` declare a `base_url` too, but `init` prints their block rather than writing it, so its messages do not offer `--base-url` for them. Passing `--base-url` with any other platform is rejected with exit code `2` rather than writing a key that platform has no node for, naming the platforms it applies to the way a provider must spell them (`generic.<instance>`, `openresponses.<instance>`) and pointing at `--endpoint` on a platform that names the same thing `endpoint`. A URL — here and for `--endpoint` — whose every `%` starts a percent-encoded octet (`https://gw.example/v1%2Fx`) is written with each `%` doubled, the container's own escape, so the platform receives it exactly as typed and `privacy.offline_only` judges it as the container will read it. Any other `%` is refused (`ContainerParameterSyntax`, `src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/`): the container would read `%v%` as a parameter reference and abort the run with `You have requested a non-existent parameter "v".`, and a lone `%` is not valid in a URL. A whole-value `%env(VAR)%` is accepted, because `StandalonePlatformConfigResolver` resolves it before the container is built. Listed in `docs/versioning.md` as part of the `init` surface.

### Changed

- **The package no longer requires the `uri` extension, which only PHP 8.5 ships.** `composer.json` required `ext-uri`; Composer accepted it on PHP 8.3 and 8.4 because `league/uri-polyfill` provides it, but tools that read the requirement without honoring that `provide` stopped on it — `composer-require-checker` aborted with `Extension "uri" does not exist`. `league/uri-polyfill` still supplies `Uri\Rfc3986\Uri` below PHP 8.5, so nothing changes at runtime, and the release build is unchanged: static-php-cli keeps listing `uri` from the polyfill's `provide`, which `.github/workflows/release.yaml` already drops.
- **A model's price is resolved once per run.** `ModelsDevPricingProvider` recomputed the catalog lookup — for a provider-qualified id without a serving-platform listing, a sort of every provider key — on every input, output, cache and `hasModel()` query of every call. Lookups are now memoized per model id (5,000 calls: 1,058 ms → 10 ms).

- **A concurrent tool-using window stops as soon as every conversation has its answer.** `ToolConversationWavefront` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/LLM/ToolConversationWavefront.php`) kept running empty rounds up to `max_tool_iterations` after every conversation had answered; it now ends the window there.

- **Long attacker runs spend less time between LLM calls.** Several steps grew with the size of the run: the attacker appended each chunk's findings by copying the whole list so far (`SequentialChunkAnalyzer`, `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer`); the confirmed- and rejected-findings preambles were rendered again for every chunk of an iteration (`ChunkContextFactory`); `VulnerabilityFactory` rebuilt its validation constraints for every reported finding; and `ChunkContextKeyDeriver` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Chunk/ChunkContextKeyDeriver.php`) recomputed the Symfony-mapping fingerprint — sorting and hashing every firewall rule, route, voter and form binding — once per chunk. Each is now done once per request, remembered per mapping instance in a `WeakMap`, or appended in place; no cache key changes.

- **`audit:diff` pairs findings without copying the list for each one.** `ReportDiffer` (`src/Command/ReportDiffer.php`) appended each fingerprint group of disappeared findings by copying the whole list so far; it now appends in place, so a diff of two large reports no longer grows quadratically. The diff result is unchanged.

- **Runs on `symfony/ai` 0.14.** `symfony/ai-bundle` moves from `^0.12` to `^0.14`, which brings the Fireworks, Together, Venice, Eden AI, TypeSafe and Higgsfield platforms. `init` writes the ones it can: `together` and `venice` take `--endpoint` and keep their default endpoint without it (`venice`'s `endpoint` node declares one, and `together`'s bridge falls back to `https://api.together.xyz`), while `higgsfield`, which needs an `api_secret`, joins the platforms whose block `init` prints rather than writes. `bedrock`'s new Mantle routes accept an `api_key`, so `init` now writes it too (see "`init` configures AWS Bedrock"). `ComposerBridgeInstaller` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Bridge/`) resolves `edenai` and `typesafe` to their real packages (`symfony/ai-eden-ai-platform`, `symfony/ai-type-safe-platform`), and a new test reads the bundle's own package checks so a future bridge cannot be installed under the wrong name. **When upgrading:** in bundle mode, raise `symfony/ai-bundle` and every `symfony/ai-*-platform` bridge your application requires to `^0.14` in the same update, since a 0.12 bridge requires `symfony/ai-platform` `^0.12`, which cannot be installed beside the bundle's 0.14; in the standalone binary, rebuild the bridge tree once with `init --force` (see "`init` holds the bridge tree to the `symfony/ai-platform` release the binary bundles" under Fixed).

- **`mcp:serve` runs on `mcp/sdk` 0.8.** The requirement moves from `^0.7.0` to `^0.8.1`, for the bundle and the standalone binary alike. Over the stdio transport `mcp:serve` uses, the server answers `initialize` with the protocol revision the client asks for when it speaks it — `2025-11-25` or an earlier handshake revision — and counter-offers `2025-11-25` otherwise; the `audit` tool keeps its name, its `path` argument and its JSON report, and now says why it failed (see Fixed). **When upgrading:** in bundle mode, an application that holds `mcp/sdk` at 0.7, directly or through another package, must let it move to 0.8.

- **`init` says what it is doing and what it wrote.** It named only the file and the variable to export, while the run sat silent for however long `composer require` took, right after the last question. It now says the bridge is downloading before it starts, and lists the provider, model and API-key variable it resolved — the three values it picks silently, including a model that defaults to `claude-opus-4-8` for every provider rather than being derived from the one you chose. A `--provider` or `--model` holding a control character, a line break or a bidirectional override is refused with exit code `2` before any of it reaches the terminal (`InitRefusal::forProviderText()`, `InitRefusal::forModelText()`, `src/Command/InitRefusal.php`), and the block printed for a hand-written platform quotes the model trimmed, as the written configuration does. `init --help` documents those defaults and shows an instance-keyed invocation; `docs/configuration.md` gains an [`init` CLI reference](docs/configuration.md#init--generating-the-standalone-configuration) with the option table and what each platform it refuses still needs.

- **`init` no longer prints a paste-ready `export` line.** `InitCommand::__invoke()` (`src/Command/InitCommand.php`) ended with `Run: export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=, then "audit <path>".` — a line whose whole purpose was to be pasted, which appends the key verbatim to `~/.bash_history` or `~/.zsh_history` the moment it is. It now confirms where the configuration landed and offers to store the key instead, naming the variable without ever teaching the leak — and when the key prompt is skipped, points at [Providing the API key](docs/configuration.md#providing-the-api-key) rather than handing over a line to paste.

- **A run with no API key now says how to get one.** `MissingEnvironmentVariableException::forName()` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/Exception/`) reported only:

  ```text
  The environment variable "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", referenced by your config, is not set.
  ```

  which names the problem and leaves the reader to find the fix. It now opens with `No API key available.`, names every way out for the variable that is missing — `auth:set --env-var=<VAR>`, an `export`, or a password-manager prefix — and points at `auth:status --env-var=<VAR>`. An unset variable behind a `base_url` or `endpoint` keeps the old message, since it holds no API key. `doctor` surfaces the same message under its `API key` check, and its green result now names the key that resolved (`Config resolves and an API key is available: sk-ant…qF4A (SHA256:…)`) instead of only asserting that one was found.

### Fixed

- **Installing the bundle with `composer require --dev` no longer breaks the host application's container.** `LockfileHashedAdvisoryCache` and, with `audit.rate_limit` set, `TokenBucketRateLimiter` took the host's `Psr\Clock\ClockInterface` service, which FrameworkBundle registers only when `symfony/clock` is a non-dev package of the application — and the bundle installed as a dev dependency, as the README instructs, brings it in as dev-only. Since 1.19.0 a fresh Symfony application failed `cache:clear` right after `composer require --dev vinceamstoutz/symfony-security-auditor` with `The service "VinceAmstoutz\SymfonySecurityAuditor\Audit\Infrastructure\Advisory\LockfileHashedAdvisoryCache" has a dependency on a non-existent service "Psr\Clock\ClockInterface".`, and kept failing for as long as no non-dev package pulled `symfony/clock` in — the platform bridge does, unless it too is installed with `--dev`. The bundle now registers its own `Symfony\Component\Clock\Clock` in `config/services.php` for both, and requires `symfony/clock`, which `src/` already used at runtime.
- **The reviewer sees the code of a finding the attacker reported as `./src/…`.** `CodeContextResolver::resolve()` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Review/CodeContextResolver.php`) compared the finding's path with `ProjectFile::relativePath()` verbatim, so a path the model echoed with a leading `./` resolved to no file and the reviewer judged the finding without its code. It now drops the leading `./` through `EchoedFilePath::normalize()` before comparing.

- **A chunk or a finding the model cannot fit no longer aborts the audit.** `FileChunker` groups files by count alone (ten per chunk), so when one chunk's files added up to more than the model's input window the provider refused the call (`prompt is too long: 213462 tokens > 200000 maximum`, `context_length_exceeded`, HTTP `413`), `TransientFailureClassifier` saw an ordinary non-transient failure, and `SequentialChunkAnalyzer` and `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer` ended the run with `NonTransientLLMFailureException` — every later chunk recorded as errored, nothing reported. The classifier now recognises the refusal (`TransientFailureClassifier::isRequestTooLarge()`, which knows the wordings of Anthropic (`input length and max_tokens exceed context limit` included), OpenAI's Chat Completions and Responses APIs, Mistral, Gemini, Bedrock, vLLM and llama.cpp, a `symfony/ai` `ExceedContextSizeException` anywhere in the exception chain, and an HTTP `413` — a gateway's `Payload Too Large` or `Request Entity Too Large`, or the status itself when the gateway answers with a body the bridge cannot make sense of: nginx's HTML page for `client_max_body_size`, which reached the run as `Syntax error` and aborted it, or a JSON body with no answer in it, such as Kong's `{"message":"Request size limit exceeded"}`, which the bridges report as `Response does not contain choices.` and the run took for an empty answer — though not the number 413 quoted in a rate-limit answer), `RetryingPlatformInvoker` raises the new Domain `LLMRequestTooLargeException` without retrying, and both analyzers hand the chunk to `OversizedChunkRecovery` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Chunk/`): it is split in two and each half analyzed through the analyzer's own per-chunk path — a half already in the cache costs no call, a half still too large is split again, and once every file of the chunk came back analyzed or from the cache the merged findings are cached under the whole chunk's own key, so the next run does not send it to be refused again (`StatusTrackingCoverageRecorder` tells which files came back that way) — and a single file the model cannot fit is recorded as errored under a `warning` log entry while the run goes on. In the concurrent path (`audit.attacker_max_concurrent` > 1) `ToolConversationWavefront` answers the refused conversation alone as a `request_too_large` response (`LLMResponse::isRequestTooLarge()`, degraded like the other answers cut short) with no retry and no sequential restart, so the other conversations of the window keep their answers and the refused prompt is sent once, not three times; a request whose estimate exceeds `audit.rate_limit.input_tokens_per_minute` (`RateLimitRequestTooLargeException` now extends the new exception) is answered the same way before it is sent. A tool-using conversation that only outgrows the model after tool results were appended ends as an `empty_content` response that keeps what it recorded, in both paths, since splitting the chunk would not change what the model asks to read. When a file refused on its own — measured as the prompt carried it, after code slicing — is under a tenth of that prompt, the prompt's fixed part — the system prompt and the project mapping — is what leaves no room, so the run stops at once with `LLMFixedPromptTooLargeException` naming the file and the remedy (a larger context window, or a higher `audit.rate_limit.input_tokens_per_minute` when that limit refused it), instead of recording file after file as errored.

  The reviewer recovers the same way in every mode. Its sequential, structured and batched modes rethrew the refusal like any provider failure, so one finding whose file did not fit in the reviewer prompt ended the run, and its concurrent window (`BatchWindowResolver`, `src/Audit/Infrastructure/LLM/BatchWindowResolver.php`) let a request larger than the rate-limit window throw out of the whole window and re-sent one the model refused through `complete()`, whose second refusal aborted the review. `SequentialReviewAnalyzer` and `StructuredReviewAnalyzer` now record that finding as errored and review the next one, the concurrent window answers such a request `request_too_large` on its own and the reviewer records that finding as errored, and `BatchReviewAnalyzer` hands a refused batch to the new `OversizedReviewBatchRecovery` (`src/Audit/Application/Agent/Review/OversizedReviewBatchRecovery.php`), which splits it in two until every part fits — logging "Reviewer batch exceeds the model input limit; it is split in two and each half reviewed on its own" — and records a lone finding that still does not fit as errored.

- **An answer cut off by the output limit or withheld by a content filter is handled the same whether the provider reports it as a stop reason or as an error.** Bridges that throw `symfony/ai`'s `MaxOutputTokensException` or `ContentFilterException` instead of returning a finish reason had their call classified non-transient, which aborted the audit. So did the other forms the bridges give the same outcome: a plain error naming the provider's own reason — the generic bridge's `Unsupported finish reason "content_filter".`, the Responses API's (Azure's too) `Responses API response is incomplete (content_filter) and contains no content.`, Cohere's `Unsupported finish reason "MAX_TOKENS".` — Azure refusing a prompt its content filter caught with an HTTP 400 whose `error.code` is `content_filter`, which the bridges turn into a `BadRequestException` that keeps only the message, and a tool call the output token limit cut off mid-way through its arguments, which the bridges raise as a `MalformedToolCallException`. `TransientFailureClassifier::degradedStopReason()` now names every one of them, the last two read back from the raw answer — its `error.code`, or its finish reason (`length` in Chat Completions, `incomplete_details.reason: max_output_tokens` in the Responses API) — by the new `ConversionFailureExplainer` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/LLM/`), and `RetryingPlatformInvoker`, `ToolConversationWavefront` and `BatchWindowResolver` answer it with the degraded `length` or `content-filter` response after one attempt, so the file or finding is recorded as errored and the run goes on; the `LLM returned a response with no content blocks` and `Tool-using loop ended with empty content response` log entries now carry a `stop_reason`. Tool-call arguments the model did finish but garbled, also a `MalformedToolCallException` that aborted the audit, are a sampling glitch and are retried. Because the run now goes on past such answers, the new `DegradedAnswerBooker` books each — an empty answer delivered as an error and every billed attempt at a garbled tool call included — against `audit.budget`, the report's token totals and the rate-limit window, at the usage its raw answer reports (`PlatformResultExtractor::extractBilledUsage()`) or, when it reports none, at its estimated input tokens, since the provider took in and bills the request; before, no spend was recorded for it. A garbled tool call is not retried once its billed attempts have spent the budget.

- **A concurrent window that ends early winds down the requests still in flight, books them, and keeps what it already finished.** When a failure ended a window of `audit.attacker_max_concurrent` or `audit.reviewer_max_concurrent` calls, `ToolConversationWavefront` and `BatchWindowResolver` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/LLM/`) left its other requests open: each then ran to completion in its destructor, which waits for the provider to finish generating and can replace the failure being raised with its own, and was never billed. Such a failure now cancels the requests still in flight and books each one's estimated input tokens against the budget, the rate limiter and the report's token totals, since the provider has already accepted it, through the `InFlightRequestCanceller` both windows share; the conversation whose failure ended the window keeps the usage it recorded. Both windows also release the rate-limit reservation of a request whose dispatch had failed and that the failure left unconsumed, which otherwise stayed counted against the window. A chunk finalized before a sibling's budget or provider abort keeps its `analyzed` status, a half of a refused chunk included, where `ConcurrentChunkAnalyzer` re-recorded the whole window as failed.

- **A finding that quotes a Markdown code block keeps its backticks.** `LLMResponse::parseJson()` (`src/Audit/Domain/Port/LLMResponse.php`) removed every ` ```json ` and ` ``` ` anywhere in the answer, so a `vulnerable_code` such as ` ```php … ``` ` reached the report as `php …`. Only a fence wrapping the whole answer is stripped now; a fenced block inside prose is still recovered.

- **A standalone config using a Symfony env processor now says so instead of asking you to export a variable no shell can have.** `%env(trim:API_KEY)%`, `%env(default::API_KEY)%` and the like were read by `StandalonePlatformConfigResolver` as a variable literally named `trim:API_KEY`, so the run stopped at `The environment variable "trim:API_KEY", referenced by your config, is not set.` The standalone binary resolves only `%env(VAR)%` and `%env(file:VAR)%`; any other placeholder that makes up a whole value in the `platform:` block now stops the run, a `--dry-run` included, with `UnsupportedEnvPlaceholderException` — "The placeholder "%env(trim:API_KEY)%" applies an env processor, and the standalone binary applies none (trim:, string:, default:, …): it reads only "%env(VAR)%" and "%env(file:VAR)%" …", or, for a name no shell can export such as `%env(1PASSWORD_KEY)%`, the rule that name breaks — and `doctor` reports it under `Configuration`. Either message spells out the processors only when every segment before the variable names a Symfony env processor, and otherwise masks the whole expression as a key is masked, however short, so a key pasted into it — an `id:secret` pair split on its colon included — is not echoed. `init` refuses the same placeholder as a base URL or endpoint instead of writing a config that cannot run. The bundle keeps Symfony's full env-processor support.

- **With several platforms configured, a key the selected `provider:` does not use no longer stops the run.** `StandalonePlatformConfigResolver` (`src/Audit/Infrastructure/Config/StandalonePlatformConfigResolver.php`) resolved every `%env()%` placeholder of every platform in `config.yaml`, so the setup [Switching providers](docs/configuration.md#switching-providers) documents — several platforms and a `provider:` naming one — broke as soon as another platform's variable was unset: with `provider: anthropic` beside an `openai:` block whose `OPENAI_API_KEY` was not exported, `audit` and `doctor` stopped at `The environment variable "OPENAI_API_KEY", referenced by your config, is not set.` With `provider:` set, every platform it does not select, and every other instance of the one it does, is now resolved the way a `--dry-run` resolves it — an unset variable becomes an unusable stand-in and the credential store is not opened for it — so `audit`, `doctor` and the `auth:*` commands all go by the connection the audit uses. Without `provider:`, every platform's settings are still required.

- **`audit --show-scanned` no longer needs a provider credential in the standalone binary.** Listing the files an audit would cover never reaches the provider, but `StandaloneApplication::needsProviderCredentials()` (`src/Standalone/StandaloneApplication.php`) only exempted `--dry-run`, so `audit . --show-scanned` stopped at `The environment variable "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", referenced by your config, is not set.` before listing a file. `--show-scanned` is exempted the same way now.

- **A relative `SYMFONY_SECURITY_AUDITOR_HOME` is refused instead of silently ignored.** `XdgConfigPathResolver` dropped a relative override and fell back to the XDG variables or `$HOME` — the very directory the override is set to escape — with no word to the user, while `docs/configuration.md` promised "any writable directory".
